He chuckled softly. "Brother Yunlong, sorry about that. Next time I'll give you a real chance to show your skills."

After sweeping the scraps of cloth into the corner, Li Banfeng stretched out on the bed and started thinking about his next steps.

Going back to Yuezhou was not an option for now. He had to find a way to get by in Medicine King Gully. Money was not a problem since he could at least eat and drink without worry. What he needed to do was improve his living conditions and buy more furniture.

He should stay in for the next couple of days. With Chu Yunlong dead, the members of the Jiangxiang Gang would be out searching for him everywhere. Still, he couldn't hide indoors forever.

What am I to do with my cultivation requirements?

At the moment, Li Banfeng had to walk twenty-five kilometers each day, as it was the requirement for a level-one Wanderlust Cultivator.

Should I just walk in this house? Li Banfeng thought and immediately got up and started pacing back and forth in the house.

However, pacing in such a cramped space was extremely difficult for Li Banfeng. If he failed to control his strength, he could crash into the wall at a speed of over thirty-two kilometers[2] an hour.

By his own estimate, if he had paced around the Pocket Dwelling for at least five hours, his face would be left bruised and swollen from all the collisions.

He could more or less control his footwork now, but he sensed that his cultivation had not improved at all. His body clearly did not recognize pacing in circles inside this tiny house as real cultivation practice. No matter how many steps he took, it wouldn't count as the cultivation of a Wanderlust Cultivator.

But it's too dangerous to go outside right now! If only I had chosen to be a pure Homebound Cultivator from the start!!! Li Banfeng complained inwardly.

Li Banfeng had no choice. This Pocket Dwelling couldn't nurture a dwelling spirit, so he would never reach level one in Homebound Cultivation. From here on, he would have to focus on Wanderlust Cultivation instead.

Of course, Li Banfeng also remembered the Peddler's warning that the gap between his Homebound Cultivation and Wanderlust Cultivation couldn't exceed three levels. Once he returned to Yuezhou, he would need to buy a house as soon as possible, even if it was just a small one, so he could nurture a dwelling spirit.

Of course, that could wait until later. For now, he had to focus on meeting the basic requirements of Wanderlust Cultivation.

Li Banfeng stepped out of his Pocket Dwelling and dug the key out from the rubble of the abandoned farmhouse.

He wore a beige suit with a black fedora and pulled the brim low as he moved quickly across the wilderness.

Dusk had already fallen, and the deserted village was silent and still. At first, he held back his pace, but once he left the village and reached the open fields, he let himself go. He ran like the wind, reckless and unrestrained. For a Wanderlust Cultivator, running was pure satisfaction.

There were no roads in the wilderness, but for Li Banfeng, every path was his to take. Running without restraint filled him with a deep sense of satisfaction and release that surged from the depths of his being.

After running for more than an hour and passing several villages, Li Banfeng felt his cultivation reacting, a sign that he had completed the day's required practice. Still, he didn't stop. He kept running, not out of dissatisfaction, but because he was hungry and needed to find something to eat.

That wasn't difficult for him. He remembered the roads clearly, every place he had passed. When he reached the entrance of a village, his eyes fell on the stone stele standing there. There were two words carved into it: Hearthstay Village.

One household still had its lights on, and Li Banfeng walked up to the door, ready to ask for a meal. He was not begging, since he intended to pay for it. This was exactly the method Chubby Qin had taught him.

Li Banfeng calmed his thoughts and carefully felt out his surroundings. Sensing no danger, he knocked on the door.

1. I wondered how he moved the bed into the house? ☜

2. 20 miles ☜

Chapter 43: Old House in the Deep Mountains

Li Banfeng knocked on the door. A man of about thirty-five or thirty-six answered. He was short but sturdy, wearing a patched vest and long trousers. He had tanned cheeks, thick brows, and thick lips. He gave off an air of simplicity and honesty.

Chubby Qin had once told Li Banfeng that there weren't many places that offered food outside the Inner Gully of Medicine King Gully. If someone grew hungry on the road, they could knock on a door for a meal. Three or five yuan was enough, and most households would not refuse.

After Li Banfeng explained why he had come, the man warmly invited him inside.

The host's clothes were plain, but his courtyard was far from small. The front yard alone was about the size of a basketball court. Inside the yard stood three tile-roofed houses and a storage shed. Though the buildings were old, they were kept spotlessly clean.

He called this courtyard the front yard because, judging by the walls and layout, there was likely a sizable backyard too.

As a Homebound Cultivator, Li Banfeng couldn't help but feel a pang of envy at the sight of such a spacious and tidy home.

"We only have some leftover rice and a few simple dishes. You have to bear with it," the man said, lighting an oil lamp in the courtyard.

A woman stepped out with a bowl of rice and a small plate of pickled cucumbers. She wore a patched, cross-collared cloth shirt. With how old she looked, she seemed to be the man's wife. Li Banfeng kept his gaze respectful and didn't linger on her.

After setting the table, she quickly slipped back inside.

Li Banfeng pulled out thirty yuan and pressed it into the man's hand, but the man waved it away repeatedly. "That's far too much."

Li Banfeng shook his hand firmly. "Take it. I feel bad for coming so late and disturbing your family."

The rice was coarse and full of husks, but Li Banfeng didn't mind at all. He was really starving and ate it in big mouthfuls with the pickled cucumbers.

The man clutched the bills with a guilty look, as though he owed Li Banfeng a debt. He slipped back inside, and soon an elderly woman with graying hair emerged.

She carried a wine jar in her arms and came to Li Banfeng with a smile. "Young man, have a taste of our homemade sorghum liquor."

Li Banfeng shook his head. "Elder, I can't drink."

He wasn't being courteous. He simply couldn't get used to the liquor here.

To a poor family, a jar of liquor was clearly precious. The old woman had even come out because of his visit. Since he didn't like it, he felt that drinking it himself would just waste their good stuff.

However, the old woman insisted and poured him a bowl. "Young man, have a drink. It's not fine wine, so don't look down on it. My son took your money, and he feels bad about it."

Since the old woman had poured it herself, Li Banfeng could no longer refuse. He picked up the bowl and took a sip. To his surprise, the liquor was rich and mellow, far better than what was sold at the bun shops.

He emptied the bowl of rice, finished the cucumbers, drank the liquor to the last drop, and let out a full, contented burp.

The old woman smiled. "Young man, have you found a place to stay? If not, you can stay here."

"I already have a place to stay. I'll be on my way now. Thank you for your hospitality.

After eating, he hurried to leave. The Jiangxiang Gang was still after him, and he couldn't risk bringing trouble to this honest family.

As soon as he got to his feet, a rush of dizziness hit him. The figures of the mother and son seemed to sway in front of him.

Did I drink too much? No! I have been poisoned!

With a smile, the old woman said, "Young man, stop running around. It's best if you stay here."

What's going on? Li Banfeng thought. He turned to bolt for the door, only to find the man's wife already there, blocking his path.

The man's wife lifted her head and slammed a fist into Li Banfeng's chin. He tried to dodge, but his body refused to listen, and the blow landed full on.

This woman is so strong! Li Banfeng thought as he staggered back two steps before crashing to the ground.

He looked closely at the woman's face. By the candlelight, he saw her features clearly at last, and a ring of dark stubble stood out around her lips.

A man! This is a shady store! Impossible! I didn't sense any danger just now!

Li Banfeng pressed his forehead, rubbed his eyes, and wiped the blood from his nose and mouth. He then slumped to the floor and closed his eyes slowly.

While his eyes were closed, he was not actually unconscious. His mind was very clear and he could hear the conversation around him clearly.

The eldest son, whose face was plain and unremarkable, glanced at the old woman. "Ma, did we really need to waste a whole jar of good wine on this Bleater? That plate of pickled cucumbers alone would've been enough."

So the pickled cucumbers were poisoned too! Li Banfeng cursed inwardly. He was feeling so much rage and frustration. He had survived Mount Bitterfog, only to stumble in a gutter like this!

Strange. I could sense malice before. At the station, I felt Chu Yunlong's malicious intent. So why did I not sense anything from this family while eating?

Are their cultivation levels unusually high? If they were that strong, why would they still resort to such vile tricks?

The old woman chuckled. "Silly boy, look closer. He's no ordinary bleater. Don't you see how he walks strangely? He's a Wanderlust Cultivator. In fact, he's a level-one Wanderlust Cultivator."

"A Wanderlust Cultivator?" The eldest son scratched his head. "Don't think I've come across one before."

The old woman said, "If you have never met one, today's your chance to learn and broaden your horizons. Wanderlust Cultivators are quick on their feet. The fact that he can use Raising a Gale on Level Ground proves he's reached level one."

"It's just that he advanced not long ago and hasn't learned to control his footwork. If I hadn't poisoned him with the wine, he would've run off and vanished in the blink of an eye. And Wanderlust Cultivators are very sensitive to danger. If I hadn't pulled my trick first, he would've rumbled us the moment he walked through the door."

"Rumbled" was gang slang, meaning the person being scammed had caught on.

So the woman did her trick first, which is why I could not sense their malice. What level is this old woman? And what ability does she have?

Hearing this, the eldest son said proudly, "Ma, I really am getting better at brewing liquor, don't you think?"

The old woman gave a sharp snort. "You advanced to level one a year and a half ago. If you still can't take down someone of considerable cultivation, how do you have the face to continue this profession?"

Through the old woman's words, Li Banfeng figured out that the eldest son was a level-one Poison Cultivator. As he listened, he tried to open his eyes, but his eyelids were unbearably heavy. He made several attempts, yet each one ended in failure.

The second son, the one dressed as a woman, spoke up. "Ma, since he isn't just a bleater, we ought to finish him now and take his head."

The second son had never seen a Wanderlust Cultivator either, but he thought that it would be best to kill off any prey with cultivation.

Hearing that, Li Banfeng's heart clenched. In his current state, he had almost no way to fight back.

He thought, Even without many methods to rely on, I have to fight. I can't let myself die here!

The old woman poked Li Banfeng with her cane. Seeing no reaction, she told her sons, "Strip him clean, shackle his hands and feet, and lock him in the prey room. We act at midnight."

Looking baffled, the second son said, "Ma, why do we have to wait until midnight?"

Yes, why wait until midnight? Li Banfeng didn't understand either. Regardless, he had already resolved to fight with everything he had.

Are they planning to strike when I let my guard down? She's overthinking it. There's no way I can keep my guard down.

"I told you to wait until midnight, so just be good boys and wait! Now, strip him clean!" the old woman snapped. The two sons stepped forward and searched Li Banfeng, taking his sickle, trowel, and wallet. They even fished out the spicy strips he had hidden in the lining of his clothes.

However, there was one thing they hadn't taken. When wiping the blood from his nose and mouth, Li Banfeng had hidden the key in his mouth.

He initially thought to unlock the Pocket Dwelling and secretly escape inside. Unfortunately, with the three of them staring, he wasn't confident he could keep the key hidden. He wasn't even sure he could turn the knob and get the door open.

At this moment, it seemed like the old woman truly didn't want to kill him.

Why did she say to wait until midnight? For what reason? And why is it that even now I can't feel any danger in this place? Li Banfeng wondered.

Chapter 44: Lend It to Me

With his hands and feet bound by iron chains, Li Banfeng was thrown into the so-called prey room. This room was nothing more than a small hostage room where they kept their captives. This family made their living by drugging people and then hurting them.

Earlier that day, before Li Banfeng was caught, a mother and her daughter had already fallen into their trap.

The mother's name was Xiao Yeci. She was about twenty-two or twenty-three and wore a long changshan robe. Even though her face was covered with dirt and wounds, her striking features and beauty still stood out. Her brows, in particular, gave her the calm, refined aura of a scholar.

Her daughter, Lu Chunying, was about twelve or thirteen. She was dressed similarly as her mother, but she didn't emanate the aura of a scholar.

When Li Banfeng was thrown into the prey room, the girl grew curious and reached out to touch him.

Her mother, Xiao Yeci, quickly pulled her back and whispered over and over in her ear, "Nan'nan, don't touch anything. Listen to me. Don't touch anything at all."

The prey room was small, barely seven or eight square meters, with a pile of firewood in the middle. Li Banfeng was thrown onto the left side of it, while Xiao Yeci held her daughter and stayed on the right, trying to keep as much distance from him as possible.

The night grew darker, and nearly an hour passed. The girl slept in her mother's arms, while Xiao Yeci tried to stay awake, but her eyelids grew heavy and she kept dozing off.

Meanwhile, Li Banfeng had regained some strength. With the mother and daughter asleep, he held the key in his teeth and turned it little by little.

Click, clack, clack.

The key pushed back stiffly, showing it had already connected with the Pocket Dwelling. However, with his neck stuck at an awkward angle, turning it three full times with just his mouth was really hard.

With his wrists locked in iron chains, Li Banfeng couldn't move his arms. He had no choice but to work the key with his teeth and tongue, turning it bit by bit. His teeth tapped against the head of the key, making faint clinking sounds

When the little girl, Lu Chunying, opened her eyes just a slit and peeked at him, Li Banfeng quickly pushed the key back into his mouth and glared at her fiercely while thinking, Nosy little girl! What are you staring at me for?!

Lu Chunying was so scared that she looked away at once. Nevertheless, because of that single glance, all his efforts earlier had been wasted.

With the poison still in his system, he had almost no strength left. He was about to spit the key out when he noticed the little girl peeking at him again.

Looking again! Li Banfeng thought. This time, he glared at the little girl even more fiercely, as if he were about to devour her alive.

Lu Chunying quickly turned her head away, but after a moment, she couldn't resist sneaking another glance. To her shock, the man was gone. He had been right there a moment ago. How did he vanish so suddenly?

"Mom, that man, he—"

"Stay still, don't say a word," Xiao Yeci said in a sleepy murmur. "Nan'nan, just listen to me. As long as we do as I say, we'll be fine."

Li Banfeng returned to the Pocket Dwelling, spitting the key into the pile of firewood before entering. He lay down for a moment before he started retching violently, vomiting all the food he had eaten the night before along with a mouthful of bile.

Then his body began to convulse, sweat gushing out with a foul, bitter stench that almost made him feel like vomiting again.

Both the sweat and the vomit carried the toxins his body was expelling.

His body was quickly forcing the poison out. This was the ability of a Homebound Cultivator. However, unlike the time at Mount Bitterfog, he was not only expelling the toxins by vomiting, he was also sweating it out.

Why is the method different this time? Is it because the poison itself is different, or has my body upgraded its way of detoxification? Li Banfeng wondered. He wanted to wipe the sweat from his face, but his hands and feet were shackled, leaving him unable to move.

Beads of sweat trickled into his eyes, making the corners sting with sharp pain.

No! I have to break free and wipe this sweat away before it poisons me again! But how can I possibly break these chains?

Currently, Li Banfeng had recovered some of his strength. He wanted to snap the iron chains, which were thicker than hemp rope, with brute strength, but the idea was ridiculous.

Are there tools in the Pocket Dwelling? Yes! A pair of pliers and a wrench I bought for fixing the gramophone!

The tools were kept in the cabinet under the gramophone. Li Banfeng crawled over. Though his arms and wrists were tied, he could still move his palms. With great effort, he pulled out the toolbox and worked at the chains with the pliers for a long time. But even after he tried to cut the chains with the pliers for a long time, the chain remained the same.

The tools meant for repairing a gramophone were too fine. It was not suitable for such rough work. Sweat streamed down his face. His eyes swelled shut from the burning pain, and he could no longer force them open.

Am I going blind?

When Chu Yunlong was made into pills, his clothes had been torn to shreds and left scattered across the floor. Li Banfeng hadn't cleaned them up back then. And now, he just wanted to rub his eyes against a scrap of cloth on the floor. He searched the floor for a long time but couldn't find a single piece.

He crawled to the bed and rubbed his eyes against the sheet, but it did nothing. Instead, the sweat only smeared across his eyelids, making the burning pain worse.

Water! I need to find water! I must clean my eyes asap!

Li Banfeng had a water bucket that he would keep by his bed. Whenever he had the chance, he would fill it to the brim. The water was always clean. He would usually drink from this bucket and use whatever that was left to wash up on the next day.

Where's the bucket? Li Banfeng thought as he searched. At last, he found it by the bed and plunged his head inside. Yet, even when his forehead touched the bottom of the bucket, he didn't sense even a drop of water.

Empty? That's impossible! I remember leaving it full! Evaporated? How could it vanish so quickly?!

Li Banfeng felt so anxious, and the more anxious and nervewrecking he was, the more he sweated.

Is there another way? Is there? Yes! As long as I can break free from these chains, there will be a way.

Suddenly, he recalled something and started wriggling across the floor like an earthworm, moving around by memory. Eventually, he wriggled to the corner of the room.

As he turned, he touched the spectacle case lying in the corner.

Thank goodness it's still here! Li Banfeng exclaimed inwardly. He strained every muscle, opened the case, and took out a Corrosion Pill.

A sharp pain stabbed his palm, but he ignored it and pressed the pill to the iron chain. At once, the metal began to corrode.

He endured for several seconds, then erupted with a burst of strength that snapped the chains around his hands. He rubbed the Corrosion Pill back and forth along the iron, and before long, every shackle binding his body broke apart.

Once he broke free, his palms split open, and blood mixed with rust trickled out. Li Banfeng was purging the toxins. His eyes still burned fiercely. He put the Corrosion Pill away, ignored his bleeding hands, and hurried out of the Pocket Dwelling.

The moment he stepped outside, the gramophone popped and crackled with steam. The record began to spin, and a song drifted out.

It was the Wandering Songtress, but with the lyrics changed.

「 From the earth's far end to the rolling sea~

Sweat may fall, but fear not…

If it stings your eyes, it will pass in a while~

My love, your bright eyes will always shine and smile. 」

Li Banfeng went back into the prey room, moving with light, silent steps. He endured the pain as he forced his eyes open and pulled the key from the firewood pile.

He glanced around, studying the layout of the prey room. One door was secured with a lock, and a small window with iron bars opened toward the back courtyard. Then he caught the sound of running water outside the window.

Water! Li Banfeng's heart leapt with joy. He opened the Pocket Dwelling once more, and with one foot inside, he tossed the key out.

When he stepped out of the Pocket Dwelling again, he was already outside the prey room, in the backyard. A well stood there, and the old woman's second son was beside it, drawing water while sharpening his saber.

The second son was almost done sharpening the saber. He wanted to draw another bucket of water to wash it clean. Just as he pulled one up, Li Banfeng snatched it away.

"Let me borrow this water, thank you." Li Banfeng even bowed as he spoke, then conveniently grabbed the man's knees and flipped him into the well.

He plunged his head into the bucket, and the cool water soothed the burning in his eyes. Only then did he realize he had been too tense. The perspiration was nowhere near as harmful to the eyes as he had feared.

After rinsing, most of the pain eased. He dumped the bucket over his head and let the water wash away all the sweat.

As Li Banfeng washed his face, the second son climbed out of the well. Clinging to the rim, he silently fixed his gaze on Li Banfeng's back.

The second son was a Martial Cultivator, a driven man who refused to accept defeat. For over a decade, he had run this business with his mother and brother, and never once had a hostage tricked him.

How could I have let my guard down and failed to hear a single step? the second son thought. He had no intention of calling for his mother or his brother. He meant to take advantage of Li Banfeng's seeming carelessness and kill the hostage with his own hands.

When Li Banfeng set the bucket down, the second son braced his arms on the rim of the well and prepared to leap out.

Li Banfeng spotted the saber lying on the ground, the one that the second son had freshly sharpened. He lifted it from the whetstone, turned, and said, "Let me borrow this blade as well."

With that, he turned and slashed the second son's throat as the man leapt out, then shoved him back into the well. He plunged into the water and floated to the surface, staring up at the sky through the opening.

He couldn't make sense of what had just happened. He had never thought a Wanderlust Cultivator could strike so quickly, or that Li Banfeng would attack without the slightest warning. While he believed Li Banfeng was focused on washing his face, Li Banfeng had been watching the well the entire time.

He wanted to call for his mother and his brother, but blood filled his mouth and water flooded his nose. He couldn't make a single sound.

Chapter 45: The Fierce Woman

Age had caught up with the old woman and she was no longer able to stay up late. At this moment, she was dozing off in the house.

Dong… Dong… Dong… The old pendulum wall clock struck nine times.

Suddenly, she felt a throb in her chest, and she jolted awake. It was only nine o'clock; midnight was still far off. So why did she feel this uneasy?

Tick-tock! Tick-tock! Tick-tock!

The pendulum of the old clock swung back and forth, and shadows slowly gathered on its gleaming surface.[1]

The prey room! Knives! Blood! Something horrible happened!

The old woman sprang out of bed and hurried to the prey room. Inside, she found the mother and daughter, Xiao Yeci and Lu Chunying, still sitting obediently where they had been. However, Li Banfeng was nowhere in sight.

"Decai! Demao! Get over here, both of you!"

The old woman shouted for her two sons. Before long, the elder, Decai, came hurrying in.

"Where's Demao?" the old woman asked.

Rubbing his eyes, Decai said, "I think he went to the backyard to sharpen the saber. You know how it's a struggle to sharpen that old saber and he might not be done even after an hour. I told you we should just buy a new one, but you wouldn't let me…"

The old woman turned around and slapped Decai across the face. "You're still rambling about that damn saber? Can't you see he's already escaped, and you didn't even notice?"

Decai held his cheek and finally realized that Li Banfeng had already escaped. He lunged forward, grabbed Xiao Yeci by the hair, and questioned, "Where did he go? When did he escape?"

Xiao Yeci screamed in a rasping voice, "I don't know! I really don't know!"

Beside her, Lu Chunying wailed, "Don't hit my mom! We don't know anything! When they threw that man in, we didn't even talk to him. We fell asleep, and when we woke up, he was gone!"

The girl was braver than her mother. She could explain things clearly. But would explaining clearly help at all?

No, it was useless. The girl didn't understand that, but Xiao Yeci knew it well. This was a family of villains. No matter what you said to them, it would never matter.

The old woman turned to her elder son. "Go call Demao. Kill these two sluts first. That Wanderlust Cultivator can't have gone far."

When Xiao Yeci heard that, she begged, "Elder Sister, please show mercy! Do whatever you want to me, but spare my child. I beg you, I beg you!"

There was a shift in her pitch as the words left her mouth.

Xiao Yeci pleaded again and again, but the old woman didn't spare her a glance. Instead, Decai felt his heart stir. "Mother, weren't we supposed to wait until midnight?"

The old woman snapped, "Something's already gone wrong! What the hell are we waiting for?!"

"Mother, this woman is so juicy and fine. There is even this fresh and pretty girl. You won't even let us brothers have a taste before you…"

Smack!

The old woman slapped him again. "What the hell have I taught you? Just a few lewd words and you've forgotten the rules? Go fetch your brother, now!"

Decai dared not say another word and hurried out of the prey room.

He had long lusted for the mother and daughter. However, his mother never allowed him to touch them. In fact, his mother had never allowed him to touch any bitches they had kidnapped in the past.

His family had never let anyone leave alive. Regardless of their gender and their age, none of the preys that stepped foot into this house made it out alive.

However, he had never once slept with the girls they kidnapped. He wasn't even allowed to touch them or speak to them. His mother always said that doing so would break the gang's rules.

His mother wouldn't allow him and his brother to interact with the women in the gang as well. Whenever one would visit, she would ask the brothers to step aside. If his mother ever heard him and his brother talk about women, they would be scolded.

What's wrong with me today? I usually just think about it, I'd never dare say it to Mother. So why did I just blurt it out? Decai patted his cheek, feeling something was off.

Then he glanced at Xiao Yeci and thought, This woman is no ordinary one. With that, he walked away sullenly.

Xiao Yeci kowtowed to the old woman. "Elder Sister, we've already given you all our money. Please, do good and earn virtue. Spare my daughter. Keep me instead. Make me a servant, make me toil for you, grind me into paste if you must. Just please, I beg you, Elder Sister, I beg you!"

Her words lingered in the prey room, echoing again and again. Before the echo had faded, Xiao Yeci spoke once more. Her voice and its echo overlapped, each word pounding against the heart.

The old woman yanked Xiao Yeci's hair and sneered viciously. "You two sluts haven't shown enough of your shameless tricks before my sons, and now you dare flaunt them at me? Do you think I can't see through your little games?

"I know what you are! You are a Mouth Cultivator. With just a few filthy words, you can hook a man's very soul. But here with me, you'd better shut that filthy mouth. I might let you die quickly. Otherwise, I'll slice every bit of that cheap flesh off you and your daughter, piece by piece!"

A Mouth Cultivator? A Daoist path like this exist? What are cultivators of this Daoist path good at? Li Banfeng thought.

Xiao Yeci wept. "Madam, with my low cultivation level, I wouldn't dare use any trick on you. Every word I spoke was sincere. Carve me to pieces if you must, but spare my child!"

The old woman sneered coldly and said, "Because of what you said, I will send your little slut of a daughter on her way first!"

With that, she yanked Lu Chunying's hair.

The little girl truly had some courage. She cried bitterly, but her hands weren't idle as they clawed and struck at the old woman.

Xiao Yeci pleaded through her tears. "Elder, I swear I was telling the truth. I am begging you! Please let my daughter live."

The old woman sneered. "If the mother dies, what use is a child left behind? Leaving her alone in this world would only mean suffering. It's better if she follows you in death."

She kicked Xiao Yeci aside and yanked harder on Lu Chunying's hair. Then she barked over her shoulder, "Decai, bring me the saber!"

The girl screamed, "Mother, save me!"

Xiao Yeci threw herself at the old woman again, but another kick sent her crashing back down.

"Decai, where's the saber?!"

"Mother, save me!"

Huh? Who just said that?

When she turned, she saw Decai standing in the doorway with the tip of the saber protruding from his chest. At this moment, his face streaked with tears.

Li Banfeng stood behind Decai and said with a smile, "Old woman, the saber's right here. I brought it for you."

At the sight of the blade sticking out of her son's chest, the old woman's eyes flushed red with rage.

Decai was a first-level Poison Cultivator, whose cultivation was on par with Li Banfeng. However, Li Banfeng had the speed of a Wanderlust Cultivator and the subtlety of a Homebound Cultivator. This was how he had managed to ambush Decai.

The old woman grabbed Lu Chunying and roared, "Let go of my son! Or I'll kill her right now!"

Li Banfeng didn't understand what she meant. "If you want to kill her, then kill her. You don't need to tell me. She's not my child."

The old woman gnashed her teeth. "Do you think I'm joking with you?"

Li Banfeng looked even more confused. "Did you say a joke? What was funny about what you said? Weren't you about to kill someone? Let's do it together. I'll join in the fun."

With that, he gripped the saber and thrust it deeper into Decai's back. The tip jutting out of Decai's chest inched farther forward.

The old woman flung Lu Chunying aside and rushed to save her son. Li Banfeng lifted his leg and kicked at her kneecap. It was a vicious move, perfect against someone charging straight at him. If it hit, it could cripple her on the spot.

However, the old woman dodged the kick and threw a heavy punch at him. The blow landed with full force, sending Li Banfeng reeling, nearly crashing to the ground.

Impossible. What level of cultivation does this old woman have? She is even faster than a Wanderlust Cultivator? And she is crazily strong!

Before Li Banfeng could think further, the old woman yanked the hairpin from her head and drove it straight toward the center of his brow.

Li Banfeng crouched down just in time to dodge.

Oh. He doesn't know martial arts. The old woman sneered inwardly. If he did, he would step back, tilt his head, or raise his weapon when the hairpin came at him. Crouching down like this… isn't that just waiting to be struck?

The old woman raised the hairpin high and drove it down at the top of his head. But she was wrong.

Li Banfeng had no intention of just sitting there and taking the blow. He had dealt with bullies all his life, often beaten until he crouched on the ground.

Crouching didn't mean he couldn't fight back. From that low stance, he drove a heavy punch into the old woman's ankle. He had put real force behind it. Normally, that would have been enough to dislocate it.

She only staggered back a step, completely unharmed. Yet that one misstep saved him. The hairpin sliced past, missing his head by a breath.

What Daoist path is this old woman part of? She can fight fast and fiercely. She is also good at taking hits. Is she a Gourmandism Cultivator?

She doesn't seem like it though. No matter how much Chubby Qin ate, he never reached the level of speed she showed. Not only was he slower, but he wasn't nearly as strong as this old woman either.

The worst thing was that every time the old woman struck, Li Banfeng sensed no danger at all. He couldn't sense her intent, so he had no way to predict her moves or choose the right response.

Obviously, he couldn't rely on just his fists and feet. Li Banfeng circled behind Decai and tried to pull the saber out.

With her skills, the old woman could have killed the mother and daughter bare-handed. The fact that she insisted Decai bring the saber meant it was no ordinary weapon.

Li Banfeng tried to pull it free, but the old woman gave him no chance. She lifted her foot and kicked him out the door, causing him to fly several meters away. Before he could get up, the old woman suddenly appeared right in front of him.

Teleportation? What level of cultivation grants this kind of ability? Li Banfent thought.

Bang!

The old woman kicked him again, flipping him through the air and slamming him flat on the ground.

This time, Li Banfeng didn't get up. With such a gap in speed, standing up would only invite another kick.

He dropped to all fours and scrambled frantically toward the wall. This time, he held nothing back. He pushed his Raising a Gale on Level Ground ability to its limit, shot to the base of the wall, and without a moment's pause, climbed straight to the top.

Just as Li Banfeng was about to leap over, the great willow tree in the courtyard lashed its branches with a crack and whipped him back down.

Controlling willow branches to strike? The old woman knows spells too! Just what Daoist path is this? Li Banfeng thought as he crashed hard to the ground. His vision was already starting to blur.

If this old woman is so strong, then why use poison on me? She could have taken me down right away. Wouldn't that have been the cleaner move?

The old woman wiped the sweat from her brow. The fight had clearly been very draining. She was no longer young, and if there had been a safer way, she would never have chosen to face him head-on.

The old woman crouched low, lifted the hairpin, and prepared herself to deliver the final strike

Dazed as he was, Li Banfeng suddenly kicked upward, driving his foot into her stomach. If he had aimed for her head, the move would have been too obvious and she would have seen it coming.

However, the kick to the stomach was subtle, and she hadn't guarded against it. The kick made her convulse and her mouth opened instinctively.

Seizing the instant her mouth opened, Li Banfeng yanked out a Corrosion Pill and hurled it into her throat. He never missed anything within five meters.

1. I thought it would be interesting to share the translation choice for this clock. Since the world setting in Puluo seems to be during the Republican era in China (roughly 1912–1949), I looked into the types of clocks that were most common in Chinese homes of that time. Considering that this household doesn't appear very wealthy, it would not have one of those tall, standing grandfather clocks. That narrows it down to either an old pendulum wall clock or a tabletop pendulum clock. ☜

Chapter 46: I Cannot Bear to Watch Him Suffer

When Li Banfeng hurled the Corrosion Pill into the old woman's throat, she happened to inhale as she convulsed from his kick to the stomach. That single breath pulled the pill straight down her gullet.

A searing pain shot through her chest. Realizing she had been poisoned, the old woman tried frantically to vomit it back out. However, Li Banfeng refused to give her the chance to vomit. He seized the moment, rushed behind Decai, and yanked the short saber out of his chest. Then he hacked at the old woman again and again.

At first she dodged with ease. But as the poison spread, her throat burned like fire. A bitter, metallic fluid surged up, and the agony soon left her gasping for breath. It was not just an unbearable pain, but that it severely affected the old woman's breathing.

As they fought, steaming hot sweat streamed down her face in dark red streaks. Her skin grew blotched and mottled, as if her face itself were turning to rust.

Li Banfeng was very familiar with this process! This old woman was clearly expelling the poison. She couldn't vomit, but she had another way to force the toxin out, one much like Li Banfeng's.

It was then that Li Banfeng realized that this woman was a homebound cultivator!

Homebound cultivators could hide their presence so completely that others overlooked them. The rickshaw driver had once said that Wanderlust Cultivators could never sense the malice of Homebound Cultivators. That was why Li Banfeng had never been able to sense the old woman's malice.

However, her two sons weren't Homebound Cultivators. So why couldn't Li Banfeng sense their malice when they fed him poison?

Li Banfeng quickly grasped the crux of the matter. It all came down to the place where this had happened. The place was this very house and this house was the key to everything.

This was the old woman's house. This was the house of a Homebound Cultivator! Within their own homes, Homebound Cultivators were protected by dwelling spirits.

The old woman had instantly recognized that Li Banfeng was a Wanderlust Cultivator. Using the power of a Homebound Cultivator, she concealed not only her own malice but also the malice of her two sons.

Is her level really that high? Is she strong enough that she could even extend her power to her two sons? Li Banfeng wondered.

The truth was that she was not at that high of a level. She was only at the first level of a Homebound Cultivation. If she had reached the second level, Li Banfeng would not have survived until now.

With her own strength, she could never have extended her powers to her sons once they stepped outside this house. As long as she remained in the house, the dwelling spirit would amplify her abilities. That was why she moved faster than a Wanderlust Cultivator, struck harder than a Martial Cultivator, and endured more than a Physique Cultivator.

How nice it is to have a dwelling spirit! Li Banfeng remarked, feeling extremely envious.

If I kill this old woman, will the dwelling spirit become mine? The Peddler had said that an unclaimed dwelling spirit could be taken over by the next master. The more Li Banfeng thought about it, the more excited he felt. He launched himself into the fight, striking at the old woman with everything he had.

With the dwelling spirit's help, the old woman tried to sweat out the toxins. However, the Corrosion Pill's poison was far too lethal, and sweating alone was much too slow a method of detoxification.

Li Banfeng had just thought of running such an experiment. He wanted to see whether a Homebound Cultivator could withstand the Corrosion Pill's corrosion inside their own home.

Of course, running such an experiment did not mean he would use himself as the subject. The most important thing was his attitude. He had to evaluate the result with the mindset that it was a scientific experiment.

Based on tonight's experiment, the result showed that if a level-one Homebound Cultivator were unable to vomit, they would not be able to withstand the corrosion of a Corrosion Pill.

By Li Banfeng's estimate, the old woman's combat strength would fall by more than half in less than two minutes.

Two minutes later, her movements grew increasingly sluggish, slower than those of an ordinary person, and strange grinding sounds came from her joints.

When Li Banfeng dodged her hairpin and kicked her in the stomach, the old woman wheezed and spat out a mouthful of reddish-brown powder.

She was rusting. Her whole body was turning to rust.

Li Banfeng quickly backed away from her. He wasn't sure if this rusting sickness was contagious.

The old woman staggered toward Li Banfeng, cursing with every step. "Little bastard, don't run. Come here. If you stay put, I'll give you a clean death. But if you dare move again, I'll slice you into a thousand pieces! You can't escape. This is my house, hehe… You've come here, and you'll never leave. You'll never leave for the rest of your life!

Her words were vicious and cruel, but deep down, the old woman was terrified.

It felt as if molten iron were being poured over her body, burning through her flesh layer by layer as it sank deeper inside.

Her son was a Poison Cultivator, and she had seen countless poisons before. Yet she had never encountered one this terrifying.

The pain was unbearable, but that was not the most terrifying part. What terrified her the most was the numbness that came after the pain. She felt her body rapidly losing sensation amidst the intense pain.

Apart from the bitter, metallic froth in her mouth, she could hardly sense the existence of her body. Vomiting was useless now.

She wanted to return to her room and listen to the sound of the old pendulum clock. However, if she went back to her room now, her son would surely die.

The old woman took a few steps forward, but each one was slower and more difficult than the last.

Rust flaked from her body, and rust-flecked foam spurted from her mouth and nose. Her graying hair turned yellow at the roots, snapping strand by strand into rusted debris that scattered on the wind.

Crack!

The old woman stumbled and collapsed heavily to the ground.

She turned her head and saw half a severed calf on the ground.

That bone had snapped in half from the rust. The flesh around it had already crumbled into powder. That was the bone she could see, but she could also feel countless others broken inside her, unseen yet undeniable.

Her body was on the verge of collapse.

She fixed her eyes on the largest room in her house. She longed for the clock to chime even once, but no sound came.

She saw Decai on the ground, convulsing and struggling, and she thought of Demao, whose fate remained uncertain.

How did it come to this? How is it possible that I am about to die at the hands of a prey? No, not just this damned prey, but also those two shameless sluts.

From the moment they stepped inside, I should have killed them. I should never have let those shameless sluts seduce my sons. My sons are mine, mine alone, and they will be mine for life…

The old woman grumbled inwardly.

"You… spare my son. I… I'll give you something good…" The old woman pleaded as she looked at Li Banfeng.

Li Banfeng glanced over at Decai, who was struggling, and said with a sigh, "I don't want to kill either, but I can't bear it."

The old woman shook her head. "I don't understand what you're trying to say."

"How can you not understand?" Li Banfeng said as he walked over to Decai with the short saber in hand. "If the mother dies, what use is a child left behind? Leaving her alone in this world would only mean suffering. It's better if she follows you in death.

"Didn't you say these exact words just now?"

"Bastard, if you dare harm my family, there will be consequences. I'm from the Jiangxiang Gang, and the Jiangxiang Gang will never forgive you!" the old woman howled

The Jiangxiang Gang again. Since it's the Jiangxiang Gang, I have to show some respect!

"Mother, save me—"

Decai's voice cut off mid-plea. Li Banfeng swung his saber and severed his head right before the old woman's eyes.

And just like that, he was forever silenced.

When the old woman saw her son's head roll across the ground, her whole body shook.

In that instant, she remembered the children she had once killed, and the way their parents had trembled just as she trembled now.

Red spots filled her vision, though she could not tell if they were rust or blood. Her consciousness faded, and her body crumbled into a heap of rust.

A cold wind swept through and scattered her remains across the courtyard.

The old woman is dead. Decai is dead as well.

With her hands covering Lu Chunying's eyes, Xiao Yeci looked at Li Banfeng in admiration even as her heart throbbed with fear.

Though Xiao Yeci had long wished for the death of this vile family, watching it happen filled her with dread. She did not know who Li Banfeng truly was, nor could she gauge his path or cultivation level.

He moves really fast so he must be a Wanderlust Cultivator, right? But how did he make that old woman rust like that?

He saved us. Surely he'll let us go! But if he doesn't… what will we do?

Li Banfeng looked a little dejected, as though he was feeling sorry for the old woman.

Xiao Yeci could see that hint of pity in him.

Xiao Yeci noticed the trace of pity in him and thought, If he can feel pity for such a villain, then he must be a good person… right? Maybe he would even be willing to help us further…

Staring at the rust scattered across the ground, Li Banfeng's expression turned solemn. He never expected things to end this way. He let out a long sigh and lamented, What a pity! Truly a pity! I could have taken the body back and refined it into a pill!

Chapter 47: True Hero of the People

Li Banfeng deeply regretted that he had lost the body he could have refined into a pill. He solemnly lamented about it for a whole minute before he started looking for the dwelling spirit.

Nothing mattered more to Li Banfeng than the dwelling spirit. Only with one could a Homebound Cultivator advance. It was the only way to overcome the undeniable gap between a Wanderlust Cultivator and a Homebound Cultivator. To him, it meant life or death.

Of course, having a dwelling spirit also meant Li Banfeng would be bound to this house. Each day, he would have to travel a set distance to fulfill the basic requirement of his Wanderlust Cultivation, then return home on time to fulfill the basic requirement of Homebound Cultivation.

It sounded contradictory, yet Li Banfeng believed he could find a way to overcome it.

What does a dwelling spirit look like? It should look human, right? They called it a spirit, so it must be in human form, Li Banfeng thought.

The courtyard held three tile-roofed houses and a storage shed. The shed was stacked with firewood and served as the prey room. Li Banfeng saw no sign of a human-shaped creature there.

He then stepped into a room in the east, where he found many jars and bottles, along with a pestle and an earthen pot for stir-frying herbs.

These look like tools for making poison, Li Banfeng thought. This must be Decai's room. Is the dwelling spirit here?

However, Li Banfeng still did not see a human-shaped creature. As he stepped out of Decai's room, he caught sight of a human-shaped creature. It was Xiao Yeci.

"Benefactor, we still haven't thanked you, eh?" Xiao Yeci said as she cautiously stared at Li Banfeng. The way he had killed moments ago still left her trembling with fear.

Behind her stood her daughter, Lu Chunying, who timidly poked her head out and glanced at Li Banfeng.

"No need for thanks." Li Banfeng brushed past the mother and daughter and walked straight into the tile-roofed house at the center.

"Benefactor, what are you looking for?" Xiao Yeci asked as she followed him.

"I'm looking for my money pouch. They took it from me," Li Banfeng replied dismissively. "You two had plenty taken from you as well, didn't you? Hurry and search. Once you find your things, hurry and leave."

"Benefactor, it's best not to barge into that house so carelessly. A dwelling spirit resides there."

She knows there's a dwelling spirit in this house? Li Banfeng thought. He didn't rush to open the door and played dumb instead. "A dwelling spirit? What's that supposed to mean?"

"A dwelling spirit is a spirit nurtured by a Homebound Cultivator. That old woman was a Homebound Cultivator. You knew that, didn't you?"

"What's a Homebound Cultivator? I have no idea!" Li Banfeng said, still playing dumb.

"A Homebound Cultivator is the kind that stays in their own house. They aren't exactly powerful when they leave home, but the moment they step inside their house, they become really invincible, eh! That is why the old woman, despite being at such an old age, was so good at fighting. When you fought her, your strikes did nothing. I couldn't reason with her either. I think she's a level-one homebound cultivator. Inside her house, even a level-two cultivator wouldn't be able to defeat her. She was able to achieve all that because her dwelling spirit was helping her, eh!"

This woman seemed to carry an unusual depth of knowledge. Though his curiosity urged him to press further, Li Banfeng kept his composure and asked, "What does a dwelling spirit look like?"

"That's hard to say. Some dwelling spirits appear as water jars, others as rice ladles, roof beams, or even old yellow dogs guarding the gate."

Li Banfeng asked in astonishment, "Has there ever been one that looked like a person, eh?"

Xiao Yeci thought for a moment. "No, I've never heard of anything like that, eh."

Li Banfeng narrowed his eyes at Xiao Yeci and asked, "Have you ever seen a dwelling spirit yourself?"

Xiao Yeci shook her head. "No, I haven't, eh."

"Then you're just spouting nonsense, eh! You Mouth Cultivators really are too much." Li Banfeng turned away, no longer willing to pay her any attention.

Xiao Yeci's cheeks flushed red. "Benefactor, Mouth Cultivator is an insult~ I am a Literary Cultivator, eh… Since one of the techniques of a Literary Cultivator is the Lotus-Spitting Technique, people would often mock us as Mouth Cultivators. All that I told you before, I learned from books, eh."

Lu Chunying poked her head out from behind and said, "My mom has read a lot of books. She's very knowledgeable, and she's amazing with her words!"

Li Banfeng replied casually, "Yeah, women who are amazing with their mouths are always impressive."

So this woman is a Literary Cultivator. What kind of skill does a Literary Cultivator have?

Before Li Banfeng could ask, the woman explained on her own. "Literary Cultivators must read a lot of books. Only through extensive reading can we learn which words to use with different people and how to act in different situations."

Li Banfeng asked solemnly, "And when you met that old woman, how did you respond?"

"Benefactor, you're making fun of me," Xiao Yeci said, lowering her head. "I have practiced the Lotus-Spitting Technique well enough, but I had never encountered a Homebound Cultivator before. I didn't expect that old woman to resist every kind of speech, be it harsh, kind, or sweet, eh!"

"I managed to persuade her two sons. If that old woman hadn't been there, they would have let me go, eh... Her younger son, Demao, the one still missing, told me his mother was a Homebound Cultivator. He also said the dwelling spirit was inside her room."

It turned out she had coaxed it from Demao. That made it far more convincing in Li Banfeng's eyes.

"Did he say what the old woman's dwelling spirit looked like?"

Xiao Yeci shook her head. "He didn't dare. He said the dwelling spirit would hear him. All he told me was that it wasn't a very large object."

Not a large object? Li Banfeng thought first of the old woman's hairpin, then of the short knife. If the dwelling spirit is something like that, can I carry it with me? And if I can carry it, does that mean I can finally resolve the conflict between Homebound Cultivation and Wanderlust Cultivation?

"If a dwelling spirit could be carried around, then a Homebound Cultivator should still be protected by it outside of their home. Wouldn't that make them impossible to defeat?" Li Banfeng asked.

"Take a dwelling spirit out of the house, eh? Nobody would ever dare try that!" Xiao Yeci's eyes widened. "Benefactor, you really don't know anything about Homebound Cultivation, eh! Most Homebound Cultivators have no idea of the true nature of their dwelling spirit, so how could they ever dare take it outside?"

"And what would happen if they did?"

"Some books say a dwelling spirit is really a vengeful spirit. Inside a Homebound Cultivator's home, it's bound in chains. But once it leaves the house, those chains vanish. And once it's free, who knows what horrors such a spirit might unleash?

"I've never heard of a Homebound Cultivator carrying a dwelling spirit outside their home. However, I have heard of dwelling spirits growing too strong and breaking free. When that vengeful spirit escaped, it slaughtered everyone in sight and wiped out several nearby villages, eh! That Homebound Cultivator fled with his entire family for over a year, but in the end he couldn't escape death. His whole household was wiped out by his own dwelling spirit, eh…"

Li Banfeng chuckled. "Good. If a Homebound Cultivator could bring their dwelling spirit out, I wouldn't know what to do the next time I met one."

Though Li Banfeng said those words, a wave of emotion welled up inside him. Regret, surprise, and a faint sense of loss all tangled together. The conflict between Homebound Cultivation and Wanderlust Cultivation was really not something that could be easily resolved.

Still, no matter what, he knew his priority was to claim mastery over the dwelling spirit. But first, he had to get the mother and daughter out of here. Li Banfeng really didn't want others to know that he was a Homebound Cultivator and he didn't want anything to go wrong while he was claiming mastery over the dwelling spirit.

"Benefactor, Decai once told me that everything they looted was stored in the cellar out back, not in the house," Xiao Yeci said.

The three of them headed to the backyard. Xiao Yeci added, "He said the cellar was under the big stone beside the well."

Sure enough, a stone block sat beside the well. Just as Li Banfeng was about to move it, he hesitated and shot a glance at Xiao Yeci. A thought crossed his mind: What if she kicks me into the cellar the moment I lift this stone? Then what?

Xiao Yeci froze for a moment, then quickly said, "Benefactor, I'm not lying. Everything I told you is true, eh!"

Whether she was telling the truth or not couldn't be confirmed for now. Still, one thought nagged at Li Banfeng. Why did I believe her so easily? Is this the trick of a Mouth Cultivator?

Xiao Yeci caught the look in his eyes and rushed to explain, "Benefactor, sometimes I can't help it. The Lotus-Spitting Technique takes effect without my intent. Please don't doubt me. How about this? I'll move the stone and go down to fetch the things myself!"

The stone block looked as if it weighed several hundred jin, and Li Banfeng doubted she could really move it. Nevertheless, Xiao Yeci spoke with confidence. "We are cultivators. Our strength can't be compared to that of ordinary people. Benefactor, just watch. I won't need your help."

Li Banfeng stood off to the side, watching coldly. Whatever tricks she pulled, he had no intention of lifting a finger.

Xiao Yeci crouched low, planted her feet, and locked her stance. She wrapped her arms around the stone and drew in a deep breath. She gnashed her teeth and poured in all her strength. Her face turned bright red, and to Li Banfeng's surprise, she actually managed to move the stone block aside.

Sure enough, a door lay beneath the stone. Gasping for breath, Xiao Yeci said, "Benefactor, I'll go down first and take a look. Chunying, stay here and don't move!"

Li Banfeng made no objection and watched as she climbed down into the cellar. Before long, she clambered back up and said, "Benefactor, there's quite a lot down there. I don't know which ones are yours, eh."

"My things don't matter. Just take your own and hurry back up!"

Xiao Yeci climbed out of the cellar with a wicker chest. Carrying a wicker chest, Xiao Yeci climbed out of the cellar. "Benefactor, all of our belongings are in here. What does your money pouch look like? I'll go back down and get it for you."

Li Banfeng lowered his gaze and studied Xiao Yeci for a moment before asking, "Why are you helping me?"

Xiao Yeci lowered her head. "How can you even ask that, Benefactor? My daughter and I owe you our lives, eh."

But Li Banfeng didn't buy it. "You're doing this just to repay me?"

"It is to repay you, but… I also have something to ask of you," Xiao Yeci said, her voice growing weaker with each word.

Li Banfeng's face remained expressionless. "What is it?"

"We mother and daughter need to go to the Inner Gully of Medicine King Gully. We don't know anyone there, and the road is dangerous. We hope Benefactor can escort us part of the way."

"No." Li Banfeng refused without a second thought. "The Inner Gully isn't far, about thirty li from here. I can point you in the right direction."

With that, Li Banfeng crouched down and sketched a simple map on the ground.

Xiao Yeci studied the map and looked back at Li Banfeng with a pleading expression. "This is our first time in Medicine King Gully, and every step of the journey has left us trembling with fear. Benefactor, please escort us. We are willing to pay, eh!"

"No amount of money will change my mind, eh! I am staying here to fight the dwelling spirit to the death, eh. The two of you should leave at once, eh!"

Xiao Yeci froze in disbelief. "Benefactor, you want to fight the dwelling spirit?"

Li Banfeng straightened his back. "What else should I do? Let this vengeful spirit roam free to harm the innocent? You've read so many books, yet you still don't understand what it means to be the noblest hero?[1]"

1. Li Banfeng is quoting Jin Yong's The Return of the Condor Heroes. (Xia Zhi Da Zhe, Wei Guo Wei Ming). It refers to someone who embodies the highest ideal of heroism, not just helping friends or seeking personal honor, but protecting the common people and even the nation. ☜

Chapter 48: The Dwelling Spirit

The noblest hero!

Lu Chunying poked her head out once more and fixed her gaze on Li Banfeng. The longer she looked, the more she felt that this young man was not only tall but upright and admirable in bearing.

"Benefactor, please take care!" Xiao Yeci called out. She then turned, gathered Lu Chunying into her arms, and rushed out of the backyard toward the gate. As she fled, one thought filled her mind: To fight a dwelling spirit in its own home… this man is utterly mad!

Xiao Yeci took her daughter Lu Chunying out of the backyard. When they reached the front courtyard gate, she turned back for one last glance and couldn't help but sigh.

With a mocking tone, Lu Chunying said, "Quit looking. You're not that pretty. The noblest hero would never look your way."

Xiao Yeci frowned. "Watch your tongue! Don't you know how to respect your elders?"

Lu Chunying gave a cold snort. "Respect for elders? Do you really think you're my mother? Fine. When we meet the Lu Family, I'll tell them the whole truth!"

Xiao Yeci gritted her teeth. "You wouldn't dare. If you do, we'll starve together, sleep on the streets together, and be sold to the brothels together. Then we'll see who cries!"

Lu Chunying frowned and said, 'If you want to pretend to be my mother, then fix that bad speech habit of yours... Stop ending every sentence with that 'eh.'"

"I don't have any bad habits in my speech! I'm telling you, don't talk nonsense, eh!"

As they talked, the two of them left the residence and stepped into a stretch of woods.

Lu Chunying tugged at Xiao Yeci's sleeve and whispered, "I remember this being a village when we came. How has it turned into a forest?"

Rustle, rustle. Whoosh.

A chilling wind swept through, and the branches and leaves swayed in a noisy chorus.

Xiao Yeci's body tensed. She pulled Lu Chunying close and said, "Something's wrong. Let's go back into the house first."

As they turned, they realized that the residence vanished. Forests spread in every direction, endless as far as the eye could reach.

"Where are we?" Lu Chunying gripped Xiao Yeci's arm.

Xiao Yeci glanced around and forced herself to stay calm. "This must be the dwelling spirit's doing. We're likely still inside the house, only unable to get out."

"Then what should we do?" Lu Chunying clung even tighter.

"Don't worry. I'll reason with him first. No matter where he comes from, if I can explain the logic clearly, he should let us go, eh!"

Xiao Yeci's expression turned resolute. As Lu Chunying stood behind her, she felt that much of her fear had eased.

Xiao Yeci tried to reason with the dwelling spirit. "Friend, I don't know where you came from, but the master of this house is already dead. You should consider where to go next. Your master committed countless evil deeds, and you were her accomplice. If the judges of the netherworld seize you and drag you into the netherworld, you will face punishment as well.

"I've read the judgment books of the Tribunal of Punishing Evil. I've seen the instruments of torment in the netherworld and the endless suffering of the ghosts there. Before, you could claim you were forced by her, that your actions were not your own. But now that she is gone, why continue down this path of evil?

"Friend, let us go. In doing so, you will erase a measure of sin and gain a measure of merit. Why would you refuse such a chance?"

Swish~

The ghostly wind gradually stilled, and the leaves no longer rustled with such menace. It seemed Xiao Yeci's reasoning was working.

Life had been hard over the years, and Lu Chunying had endured her share of grievances, quarrels, and distance with this woman, who was not truly her mother. Yet whenever danger came, Xiao Yeci never hesitated to step in front of her and protect her. At this moment, Lu Chunying believed that Xiao Yeci was the most dependable person in the world.

"Come, follow me!" Trusting her instincts, Xiao Yeci strode forward, with Lu Chunying close by her side.

They had barely taken two steps when a willow branch lashed out at lightning speed, striking both Xiao Yeci and Lu Chunying across the face with a sharp snap.

Xiao Yeci covered her face and gritted her teeth, forcing back her tears. However, Lu Chunying, who was still a child, couldn't stop herself, and tears ran down her face.

Xiao Yeci took out a handkerchief and carefully wiped the streak of blood on Lu Chunying's face. Her chest tightened with pain as she murmured, "Nan'nan, don't cry. I just chose the wrong way. We will go left. Now, keep by my side."

With streaks of blood on their faces, they turned left into the forest and pressed on against the night wind. After only a dozen steps, another willow branch lashed through the air and struck them both again.

This time, tears fell from Xiao Yeci's eyes, and Lu Chunying sobbed loudly. Each now bore two wounds on their faces, the streaks crossing to form an X.

"Maybe I was wrong. Let's go right this time. Come on, stay close to me!" Xiao Yeci gripped Lu Chunying's hand firmly.

"Mom, you go ahead. I'll just follow behind you." Lu Chunying pulled her hand free. In that moment, she took back what she had just thought.

Xiao Yeci isn't dependable at all! she thought.

Li Banfeng stepped into the old woman's room and examined the furnishings one by one. There was a bed, a table, two chairs, and a standing cabinet.

The furnishings were simple. Li Banfeng scanned them one by one, trying to guess which might be the dwelling spirit.

An old tabletop pendulum clock sat on the table. It carried no trace of spirit and was clearly not the dwelling spirit.

Next to the clock was a teapot. It carried a lively air, making Li Banfeng wonder if it was the dwelling spirit.

A sewing kit rested next to the teapot. Its spools of thread were arranged tidily, and the needles set in with exact care. It gave off the air of a dwelling spirit.

Beside the sewing kit lay a rolling pin. The flour dusted across it was evenly spread, and it too showed faint traces of spirit.

So which one is the dwelling spirit? Li Banfeng wondered, feeling conflicted. I'm a homebound cultivator. There should be some resonance between me and a dwelling spirit.

Dong!

The pendulum clock gave a single chime, and Li Banfeng glanced over. The hands pointed to half past nine.

This type of traditional tabletop pendulum clock chimed once on the half-hour and struck the full count on the hour.

For example, at nine o'clock it would chime nine times, but at half past nine it would chime only once! That earlier chime had interrupted Li Banfeng's train of thought as he searched for the dwelling spirit, leaving him rather displeased.

He shot the old clock a glare before turning his eyes back to the rest of the room.

At last, his gaze settled on a feather duster. Its colors were vivid, and its feathers sleek and soft. Of all the items, it seemed to hold the strongest sense of spirit.

Li Banfeng picked up the feather duster, preparing to use his Homebound Cultivator abilities to probe deeper. Suddenly, the old pendulum clock struck.

Dong, dong, dong…

The old pendulum clock struck ten times in a row. The sound was so loud it completely pulled Li Banfeng's attention away.

Ten times? Li Banfeng thought, staring at the clock again. Both the hour and minute hands pointed to ten o'clock. It was half past nine just now. How did it become ten in the blink of an eye? Could it be that…this old pendulum clock is broken?

Just moments ago, Li Banfeng had been thinking that he needed a timekeeping tool in his Pocket Dwelling. He had even considered moving this old tabletop pendulum clock inside. But judging by the situation, he couldn't take this clock anymore.

Li Banfeng tried communicating with the feather duster for a while, but it gave no response.

Next to it was an iron mooncake tin. Though its paint was flaking with age, the surface gleamed and looked perfectly clean.

Dwelling spirits liked cleanliness. Could it be inside this tin? Li Banfeng wondered. He lifted the lid firmly and found only a stack of old newspapers, yellowed with age and brittle enough to crumble at a touch.

Surely a dwelling spirit wouldn't live in newspapers? Li Banfeng thought. Yet the moment he lifted one sheet, even before unfolding it, he sensed a real response.

He felt the printed words on the newspaper reaching out to him. The tiny letters, no bigger than a fly's head, writhed like little worms, trying to burrow into his fingertips.

Could it really be in the newspaper?

Tick-tock! Tick-tock! The pendulum's sound grew sharper, each strike tugging hard at his heartstrings.

Li Banfeng laid the newspaper down and looked at the golden pendulum, which in the glow of the candles cast faint, wavering images.

At first the images were blurry, but they gradually grew sharper. To Li Banfeng, it felt like watching an old film with a faded yellow backdrop playing across the pendulum.

A beautiful woman in a qipao stood before a mirror, painting her brows. A man appeared behind her, took the pencil from her hand, and gently traced her eyebrows for her.

After drawing her brows, he moved onto lining her lips. Once he had drawn them, he bit them. From her lips, his bites wandered elsewhere. The two then came face to face, biting one another for a long while, until the man finally circled behind her.

The ticking of the pendulum grew clearer and more insistent.

The man had been holding the woman's shoulders, but in the midst of their movements, his left arm slipped around her neck while his right hand clamped onto his left.

He pressed his face against hers, drove his left shoulder into the back of her head, and forced her neck forward.

A rear naked choke! Yet not the version that left someone "singing" from lack of air. This was the brutal kind, with the attacker gouging at the eyes.

While Li Banfeng was still examining how hard the rear naked choke was to execute, the woman in the vision froze. She had already been choked to death.

The man pulled out, put on his clothes, and disappeared from the scene. The woman's corpse remained in the room. The image seemed frozen, though light and shadow kept shifting across it.

Time passed from morning to dusk, from day into night, and her body rotted slowly, alive with maggots.

Then, one day, the image showed her corpse being discovered and carried away from the center.

People moved in and out, though it was unknown who they were. They cleared everything, taking piles of jewelry and wads of cash.

The story seemed to finish once the scene was cleared away, leaving nothing behind.

What is the meaning of this story? Why did this old pendulum clock let me see this story? As Li Banfeng pondered, he realized the story was not over yet!

In the empty room, a woman appeared. It was the very one who had been strangled to death. She held a comb in her hand, smiling as she combed her hair.

The woman who had once been strangled appeared again inside the house, fixing her hair and make up.

Though the mirror was gone, she faced Li Banfeng directly, combing her hair with an alluring smile that felt strangely close and familiar.

This must be the dwelling spirit, right? Li Banfeng thought.

Suddenly, the woman disappeared and a man appeared in the scene.

Though he was dressed differently, Li Banfeng still recognized him as the murderer who had strangled the woman.

He seemed to be searching for something, running his hands carefully across the floor until he found a hidden compartment. From within, he retrieved a short saber.

Li Banfeng recognized the weapon. It was the short saber the old woman had cherished greatly. That very weapon was now fastened at his waist.

The man's eyes lit up with excitement. This was what he had been searching for all along.

He brushed the blade with his fingertips, but the hilt suddenly convulsed and wrenched itself from his hand. The short saber rose into the air, hovering before him.

The man was horrified and scrambled backward on all fours. Still, he was too slow.

With a flash, the saber shot forward, slicing his throat. A spray of crimson blood erupted.

Clutching his throat, the man struggled in desperation. His thrashing resembled the woman's final struggle before death.

The woman's figure slowly emerged once more in the scene. With a sweet smile, she calmly resumed fixing her hair and makeup, not paying any attention to the man convulsing on the ground.

Even as she touched up her makeup, she kept staring at Li Bangeng with this chilling sweetness in her eyes.

Just focus on your makeup! Why do you keep staring at me? Every crime has its culprit and every debt its debtor. I wasn't the one who killed you!

Li Banfeng tried to look away, but suddenly the woman set down her comb, picked up something resembling a key, and reached it toward him.

What? Are you about to attack me? If this woman is the dwelling spirit, do I need to defeat her to claim mastery over her? Li Banfeng thought, remaining on high alert. Then he saw the woman lift a glass cover and slowly turn the key.

He watched closely for a long time before realizing that it was not a real key at all. It was the winding key for the old tabletop pendulum clock.

The woman had not been doing her hair and makeup while facing Li Banfeng. She had been doing it while facing the clock.

Back then, not everything had been cleared out of the woman's house. One thing had been left behind, and it was this old tabletop pendulum clock.

Li Banfeng had not witnessed the scene like an audience watching a film. He had seen everything from the perspective of the clock itself.

Once her brows were drawn and her lips painted, the woman raised her head. Her red lips opened gently as she asked in a tender voice, "Am I beautiful?"

It was the first time Li Banfeng had heard the woman's voice, and he wondered, Is she asking the clock or me?

When the woman asked, her gaze brimmed with eager anticipation.

After a moment of thought, Li Banfeng answered, "Beautiful."

The woman smiled, clearly satisfied with the answer. Then she asked again, "Do you know my name?"

Li Banfeng thought for a moment, then lowered his gaze toward the iron mooncake tin. He then reached into the tin and pulled out an old newspaper.

On the second page was a news article and every letter on that news article quivered continuously.

The headline read: Renowned Beauty Luo Yuni Tragically Strangled in a Secluded Residence.

The woman asked again, "Do you know my name?"

Li Banfeng nodded. "I think I do."

"Look at me. Keep your eyes on me. Say my name aloud, and I will belong to you," the woman said with a smile that had turned sweeter and more beautiful.

Chapter 49: The Famous Beauty

No wonder the old residence carried an antique charm along with an air of grandeur. It had once been the residence of the famous beauty Luo Yuni located outside the city.

This woman was Luo Yuni, the famous beauty once reported in the newspapers to have died tragically in her own residence.

Is she the old woman's dwelling spirit? Where did the newspaper come from? And what connection does this Luo Yuni have to the old woman? Li Banfeng wondered.

When Li Banfeng did not utter her name, Luo Yuni seemed to understand what he was worried about and asked, "Do you want to know what happened afterward?"

Clang! Clang! Clang!

As Luo Yuni continued her story, the tabletop pendulum clock struck eleven times. With each swing, the scene changed. The man who had murdered her now lay dead on the floor, while she sat beside his body, calmly fixing her hair and makeup.

The man’s body was taken away, but before long, he came back. He appeared beside Luo Yuni, holding an eyebrow pencil as if he wished to draw her brows. It was clear this was no longer the living man, but his ghost.

Luo Yuni did not spare him a glance. With a casual wave of her hand, she cast him out the window. There happened to be a willow tree outside, and his ghostly figure ended up hanging from its branches.

Luo Yuni went on combing her hair and touching up her makeup. Even as the days slipped by, her smile stayed as alluring as ever.

Day after day, the man's ghost dangled from the willow tree, gazing at Luo Yuni with an unbroken obsession.

After what felt like ages, a young couple moved into the house. The husband wore a white shirt and black suspenders. Each day he left early and returned late, giving the impression of a man with a proper job.

While the wife busied herself with chores, she never noticed the other woman in the house. That woman sat before the old pendulum clock, carefully tending to her hair and makeup.

The wife could not see her. In truth, if Luo Yuni chose not to reveal herself, Li Banfeng would never see her.

The days had been simple and sweet. Then one day, the husband saw Luo Yuni combing her hair and doing her makeup, and from that moment, he no longer found life sweet. He began to feel that his wife was not truly beautiful. When compared to real beauty, it seemed she might never have been beautiful at all.

Luo Yuni looked at the man with pity, then gently extended her arm and handed him the comb. Overcome with excitement, he carefully took it from her hand, and to comb her hair became the greatest achievement of his life.

Li Banfeng could not fathom the man's thoughts, nor the obsession and rapture on his face.

The man combed Luo Yuni's hair over and over, unwilling to leave a single strand untouched. Only when she grew weary and cast him a glance did he finally set the comb aside.

After setting down the comb, the man picked up an eyebrow pencil, intending to draw Luo Yuni's brows. However, she shook her head, and slowly faded away.

She was gone, leaving the man clutching the eyebrow pencil. He stood in the same spot, appearing lost and helpless. From then on, he spent his days staring blankly at the pendulum clock, indifferent to his wife's complaints and tears.

One day, he plunged the eyebrow pencil into his temple, stabbing himself again and again. With every stab, his face contorted in pain beneath a grotesque smile. Finally, he saw Luo Yuni again. He believed that as long as he kept stabbing the pencil into his temple, he could be with her.

Clutching the bloodstained pencil, he longed to draw Luo Yuni's brows. His wife saw this and fled the house in terror, but she never made it past the courtyard. The ghost hanging from the willow lashed out its branches, binding her and dragging her back inside.

No wonder the willow trees never let anyone leave the residence. They were all Luo Yuni's slaves, souls she had hung upon the branches.

The husband stabbed himself to death with the eyebrow pencil. The wife was bound by the willow branches until late night before Luo Yuni cut her throat with the short saber. She died, and the short saber was drenched in blood that vanished almost at once.

The scene froze, and Luo Yuni said gently to Li Banfeng, "This saber loves drinking blood at midnight. The more it drinks, the sharper it becomes. It took me three years to uncover its profound secret, while that old hag spent thirty and still failed to see through it. Yet on our very first meeting, I revealed this secret to you. Is that not proof of my sincerity?"

Luo Yuni's smile grew even more captivating, yet Li Banfeng's gaze was no longer on her smile.

"You're lying," Li Banfeng said as he shook his head. "That old woman knew the secret of this blade. She didn't rush to kill me but kept me until midnight so the blade could drink my blood."

Luo Yuni smiled. "That was because I only told her the secret last night. Didn't you notice? Even her two sons didn't know why they were told to keep you until midnight. It was because the old woman hadn't had the time to tell them.

"That is how foolish that old hag was. If I hadn't told her, she would never have uncovered the secret of this saber. Cultivating at her side was nothing but an insult and a torment to me."

Li Banfeng asked, "Then why did you choose her?"

Luo Yuni shook her head. "I didn't choose her. She chose me. She was the only Homebound Cultivator I ever encountered, and she called me by my name. Before I met her, I was nothing more than a vengeful spirit. When she summoned me, I became a dwelling spirit."

Li Banfeng's eyes lit up. "How does one summon a dwelling spirit?"

Luo Yuni arched her brow. "Don't you already know? Aren't you a Homebound Cultivator as well?"

Li Banfeng raised his brows in turn. "You could tell?"

Luo Yuni cackled. "How could I not? In my house, not a single move of yours can escape my eyes. You have a very special house. A house that ordinary people and even ordinary dwelling spirits cannot see. But I am no ordinary dwelling spirit. I can see your house and I know you have a key to open that residence. It was only because you returned to it that you were able to quickly expel the poison Decai gave you."

Hearing this, Li Banfeng had a sudden realization. He realized that unless it was truly necessary, he should never again step into the home of a Homebound Cultivator so easily.
